## 2026-10-24, #2141 incremental ClassFilter membership discovery

- Worktree `D:/Mudcrab/worktrees/classfilter-incr`, branch `phase2/classfilter-incremental`, base `d524ae21d`. Moving visibility filters derive hidden CPU slots and GPU keys from saved pairs plus the union of raw membership deltas and a small requested-hidden-set symmetric difference. Full raw CPU/GPU membership sets and a separate requested hidden set initialize on the general path, then move between saved frames with only touched keys updated. Effective `previous_hidden` remains the event baseline; requested-but-absent entities do not become changes every frame. Scratch delta keys are normalized; original event vectors, renderer precedence, sorted-unique synthetic events and exact restore remain intact. Length or pair mismatch and duplicate CPU keys reject incremental selection. Empty-hidden no-op frames discard saved caches. Large requested-hidden-set changes also use the general path.
- Confirmed Bevy 0.19.0 contract: `vendor/bevy_render-0.19.0/src/view/visibility/mod.rs:201-249` diffs CPU membership by `MainEntity`, allowing silent renderer-pair replacements. The collector sorts CPU entries without deduplicating (`:421-427`). Locked `bevy_pbr-0.19.0/src/render/mesh.rs:1675-1704,2263-2351` includes CPU/GPU migrations and render-layer membership changes; GPU removals append after CPU removals without a final sort, and additions are sorted without deduplication. Local scratch normalization and separate cached CPU/GPU membership avoid assuming globally sorted, unique, source-specific deltas. Overlapping CPU/GPU entries remain supported with CPU pair precedence for synthetic events.
- Discovery now performs one CPU binary search per changed union key, with linear requested-set comparison, hidden-pair verification and merging; it does not sort or binary-search every retained hidden slot. Whole apply/restore still includes the existing linear CPU compaction/reconstitution. Cache memory is proportional to raw CPU/GPU membership plus the requested hidden set. Runtime counter: `stock_visibility/general_path`; test counters distinguish replay, incremental and general paths. No dependency, renderer setting, cap, scheduling or public-interface change.
- Added a seeded oracle test with 256 cases of 64 frames (16,384 frame comparisons), forcing a fresh general search each frame and comparing every class field, effective hidden set and both restores. Cases include CPU/GPU churn and overlap, hidden-set changes and absent keys, unsorted GPU removals, duplicate CPU keys and silent renderer replacements. Focused tests cover moving/small-change path selection, duplicate events with differing overlapping renderer pairs, large-change fallback and empty-hidden invalidation. The moving regression failed before implementation: three general selections instead of one. Independent Codex review identified repeated general work on absent-rich requests; a new moving regression failed with five general selections instead of one while the other 16 visibility tests, including the oracle, passed. The separate requested-set cache corrects that cause without changing effective event state. All 17 visibility tests pass on final source. A fresh independent Codex review of the full diff against the base found no actionable finding, confirmed the requested/effective-set invariant and found no mod/worldspace-specific shortcut; reviewed source blob `0004e4793b15971bf804106a0c8bcf8d421970bf`.
- Required final gated checks passed on the reviewed source: `cargo fmt --all -- --check`; `cargo clippy -p engine --all-targets --all-features -- -D warnings`; `cargo nextest run -p engine --all-targets` (**3,125 passed, 13 skipped**, three slow, **247.771 seconds**). ## 2026-10-24, task 2138: indexed cluster draws and shadow diagnostics

- Phase 2 branch `phase2/cluster-indexed-draws`, owned worktree `D:/Mudcrab/worktrees/cluster-indexed`, based on `91c23bbd296074a1516e3de11c94849de1ec66c6`. Indexed draw commit `afd3ba56c8af1773de5aadb4765916e7b15e8ec7`; the following commit contains shadow diagnostics and this handoff. Nothing was pushed or otherwise published.
- Cluster uploads widen the existing local byte indices once into an index-only Uint16 GPU pool. Uint16 is wgpu's smallest portable format and covers every original local index; pooled addressing uses 32-bit first_index and base_vertex zero. Four-byte padding preserves queue-write and next-mesh alignment. Five-word indexed arguments, buffer limits, group offsets and both stable scatters agree. The existing selection scratch carries first_index without increasing the scatter storage-binding count beyond eight.
- Shared indexed indirect rendering covers forward, prepass, opaque and masked shadows, terrain and optional far terrain. Terrain material/prepass files are fragment stages and inherit the changed vertices. Triangle order, winding and vertex source selection are preserved. Missing indirect-count support retains the fixed-count path with cleared zero-index arguments. The locked Metal backend implements indexed indirect sequences with 20-byte stride; no CPU count readback is introduced. Runtime Metal and performance behavior were not exercised.
- The full published bevy_pbr 0.19.0 crate is vendored (153 published files, approximately 4.19 MB), preserving every published byte except src/render/light.rs. Cargo cannot replace this private module alone; the complete tree preserves sibling modules and embedded shader/LUT paths. Root Cargo wiring excludes PBR from workspace membership, retaining the engine's features and dependency optimization. Cargo.lock changes only PBR's registry-to-path resolution. Licenses, archive checksum and upstream revision remain in the vendor tree/README.
- The shared shadow helper brackets directional cascades, point-light faces and spot-light passes with optional paired begin_pass_span/end_pass_span calls, after existing early returns and around the existing handled-error path. Early/late suffixes distinguish stages. Direct calls avoid the owned-label leak in Bevy 0.19's PassSpanGuard::end; no dynamic label is allocated without a recorder. Existing query capacity is unchanged (256 timestamps, 128 statistics queries per frame); exhaustion can omit measurements. Repeated camera labels aggregate pass observations, not per-frame totals or stable light identities.
- benchmark.json now exposes elapsed shadow GPU paths under gpu_passes.passes and vertex/fragment/clipper paths under gpu_passes.pipeline_statistics. scene.fragment_shader_invocations is added beside existing counters. Timestamp and statistics gates are independent; unavailable measurements stay absent, and a measured depth-only fragment count of zero remains valid. Existing sampling/history cursors are unchanged.

Proof: 6/6 snow poses over two cells and 11/11 Tamriel exteriors from Riften to Solitude in one launch each (twice), no new crash log. One session per worldspace is now the default; `--shots-per-session` / `--session-radius` restore caps. Plugin log shows per-second want / player / camera / focus / pause / open menus while a shot settles.
- **Shared setup.** `run_skyrim.py` takes `C:/Modding/MO2-capture/capture.lock` after it holds a gate slot and only then copies the staged ini and route (a queued run cannot overwrite a running one); it elevates MO2 on WinError 740. Hand-made launches (RenderDoc) bypass the lock.

## 2026-10-14, Build: O1 stored BC7 textures turned into a zstd decoder switch (T3 thread)

Local branch `phase1/stored-bc7-textures` (worktree `C:\Users\Brannock\.t3\worktrees\mudcrab\phase1-stored-bc7-textures`), commits `45bdefac` and `1f96af22` on main 268f9884. Nothing published; the user decides.

- **Premise was stale.** A default conversion already stores native BC blocks with zstd level 6 (PR 53, PR 114). Of 36,426 `.ktx2` files in `SkyrimConverted-p2main-v24-doors`, 99.96% are native BC (BC3 20.3k, BC7 10.2k, BC1 6.3k, BC2 172); only 13 are UASTC (11 DLC01 cubemaps and `effects/noisevolume` with its sRGB twin). No BC4/BC5/BC6H and no size that is not a multiple of 4. The 56.5 ms transcode figure in `texture-load-cost.md` predates those PRs.
- **What changed.** Root `Cargo.toml`: bevy feature `zstd_rust` (ruzstd) replaced by `zstd_c`; `Cargo.lock` gains the `zstd` dependency of `bevy_image`. Two converter tests in `texture.rs`: native BC with sizes that are not multiples of 4, and BC4/BC5/BC6H/BC7 in normal and data (True PBR style) slots. No stored byte changes: no schema bump, no reconversion, paths and load-order winners untouched.
- **Measured.** Decode only, 1,500 real textures: ruzstd 7.04 ms mean, C zstd 3.11 ms (2.27x). ## 2026-10-14, Research: O2 occlusion culling proof (T3 thread "Research: O2 occlusion culling proof")

Branch `plus-occlusion-proof` (commits 3c082e07 counter and probe, 9738ebc7 Aabb guard, local, not pushed). Nothing published, nothing merged into Plus. Note: `local/research/occlusion-o2-2026-10-14.md` (gitignored, row in `INDEX-mudcrab.md`).

- **Proof and counter.** `gpu_culling_active` is true on Riverwood, `travel` and `check`. New `--cull-counter` (`crates/engine/src/cull_counter.rs`) reads each view's finished indirect draw commands back and reports submitted, drawn and rejected instances per view and phase in `renderer.json` and the profile summary; `--no-main-occlusion` turns the main camera's OcclusionCulling off for the A/B. fmt, clippy `--all-features -D warnings` and 1,366 engine tests pass.
- **Numbers.** Occlusion rejects 95.5% (Riverwood), 91.1% (`travel`), 86.3% (`check`) of submitted opaque instances; with it off the GPU frustum test rejects nothing the CPU test did not. It saves about 0.22 ms of GPU time per frame and costs no measurable frame time: in the window that was checked quiet before and after every run, `travel` is 164.8 fps off and 163.4 on, Riverwood 176.9 off and 183.2 on (spread 172-202). An earlier window showed `travel` 6% slower; it is dropped because another thread's hold overlapped a later window of mine and its absolute numbers are 14% higher. Recommendation: keep OcclusionCulling on the main camera.
- **Overlap incident and fix.** My Riverwood 5-run window (about 20:20-20:36) overlapped the cluster thread's `cluster-ab` hold (set 20:22:37). The gate refused a new hold only when the new one was timing-named; `~/.mudcrab/build-gate/build_gate.py` now refuses any new hold while a timing hold is set (backup `.bak-2026-10-14-o2`; `--allow-overlap` stays). My timing script now re-checks its hold, other holds and any engine.exe before and after every run and marks the run INVALID. Overlapped data was not used.
- **Aabb guard.** `AabbGuardPlugin` (`crates/engine/src/aabb_guard.rs`, commit 9738ebc7) logs an error when a mesh has `NoAutoAabb` and no `Aabb`, because Bevy's GPU culling never draws it.
- **Mod check.** `examples/occlusion_mod_probe.rs`: alpha-tested foliage cards are safe; a mesh with a wrong `Aabb` vanishes only with occlusion on (needs `NoAutoAabb`, otherwise Bevy recomputes it); a mesh with no `Aabb` is never drawn at all. The engine has no vertex-displacement shader yet.
- **B1.** Trial merge `D:/Mudcrab/worktrees/o2-b1` (B1 portal merge + counter): cull rate changes 0.2 points, batch sets 29 to 9 and 32 to 4. Bevy 0.20, main and open PRs: no occlusion change ("in 0.20: no").
- **R1.** Not folded; shares the timing window and consumes `renderer.json` `cull`.
- **Disk.** The user approved deleting `D:/Mudcrab/builds/portal-target` (39 GB, Portal Dev, newest file 2026-10-11) to make room; the portal checkout's build folder junctions will rebuild cold. My folders: `D:/Mudcrab/builds/o2-target`, `o2-b1-target`, worktree `D:/Mudcrab/worktrees/o2-b1`, scratch `C:/Mudcrab-builds/o2-out` (all with OWNER files; delete with the user's yes when O2 closes).

## 2026-10-13, VG6: cluster LOD payload ADR, accepted (T3 thread "cluster payload ADR")

ADR-0013 (cluster LOD payloads for opaque statics) is accepted by the user as the contract for the cluster LOD prototypes. Queue row VG6 set done. Nothing was published; no upstream issue or comment is planned until the user has seen the demo.

- **What it says.** Two payload kinds: per-mesh `.mcl` keyed by the GLB `output_hash`, per-block `.mci` on the LOD grid. One builder identity. Optional tables, no schema bump (world DB 7, converter 24). The converter picks one mode per package before conversion: `--lod-mode auto|cluster|baked`, `auto` runs a GPU capability probe; `cluster` skips baked object LOD for eligible primitives; `baked` is today's path. Clusters own eligible objects at every distance (no near/far swap); near cells wait for their cluster block. Default stays `baked` until the comparison says go.
- **Proof the demo must show** (ADR "Prototype proof"): conversion time per stage cold, warm and after one plugin change, bytes, converter memory, image diff, frame time on `lod-far` and `lod-flythrough`, pop-in flythrough.
- **Found on the way.** On plus the engine draws no object LOD or tree billboards (the converter publishes them, nothing reads them), so the baked side of the comparison must be built too. New rows N2 (baked engine prototype) and N3 (comparison report).
- **Threads started by the user's yes.** Converter cluster stage (row VG8), engine cluster prototype (row VG7), baked object LOD in the engine (N2). N3 waits on those three.
- **Open in the ADR.** Which upstream issue holds the project schema; #185 stable-ID decisions (stable reference IDs are required for a shipped `cluster` package); the change to Chef's baked chunk builder (#106, exclusion set); triangle threshold; texture binding and parallax/glow; a package moved to a weaker PC.
- **Branch.** `phase1/vg6-cluster-payload-adr`, head b19c04a6, docs only, local (not pushed). Comparison table: `local/research/fun/lod-comparison-table.html`.

## 2026-10-11 night, VG5: automatic plain LOD chains vs cluster LOD (T3 thread "VG5 Compare automatic plain LOD chains with cluster LOD")

Research spike, nothing published, no converter or engine change, no cargo on the repo (the harness is a standalone crate in `C:/Modding/Mudcrab/scratch/p1-vg5-lod-compare/`, built outside the gate because it has no Bevy and both drives were under the gate's 15 GB floor; D: was under 1 GB at claim time). Queue row VG5 set done.

- **What was done.** Chef's ask on issue #203 (compare the cluster path with automatically generated conventional LODs at matched quality). A Rust harness builds, per primitive, a discrete meshopt LOD chain with clusterlod's own simplifier options and the clusterlod DAG (VG3 crate, read only), and selects both at the same projected error (1 px, 1600x900, 45 deg fov) over eight distance bands. Sample: VG2's 79 meshes, every static placed in Whiterun city and its 9x9 Tamriel block, and 10 synthetic mod-style meshes (833 meshes, 3,621 primitives, 2.13 M triangles, 0 failures). A software rasteriser sweep (48 distances, 14 meshes) measured image difference and popping; a world-database join gave draws and resident bytes for the Whiterun scene. Everything by DeepSeek workers (coders, analysis, websearch) with an independent DeepSeek review whose "fix first" findings were applied (the first chain had Prune and no Permissive, unlike clusterlod; byte columns not like for like; open-edge column no crack detector).
- **Result** (`local/research/fun/vg5-plain-lod-compare-2026-10-11.md`, indexed). At matched 1 px error the whole-mesh chain draws fewer triangles from 4,096 units out and 2 to 3 x fewer beyond 35,000, because the DAG's floor is one 128-triangle cluster per primitive (38 % of primitives never simplify in it). The DAG wins only on big meshes at mid distance (a quarter of the over-1,000-triangle primitives at 8 to 16 k units). Build 1.4 vs 0.5 to 0.8 s per M triangles; whole-asset bytes 120 % vs 118 % of today; draw units 1 per primitive per instance (chain) vs 1 indirect command per visible cluster per instance (DAG). Popping at 1 px is small for both on real meshes (DAG switches more often in smaller steps). Both fail thin geometry and disconnected clutter the same way; the chain lost a thin slab once that the DAG kept. Whiterun opaque statics from the gate: today 252 k tris / 693 draws, chain 105 k / 693, DAG 117 k / 1,494 commands; draws are instance-bound either way, so settlements need per-cell HLOD (Chef's point).
- **Recommendation.** Build the automatic discrete chain in the converter first (every material, every size, far bands, today's instancing, smaller engine change), add per-cell HLOD proxies for settlements, keep the cluster DAG as a scoped mid-distance experiment for big opaque meshes with a plain-chain tail below the root, decided on engine measurements (VG7, VG0a). Either path needs a thin-geometry coverage guard, Permissive on, welding, and an alpha-silhouette check for cutout.

## 2026-10-11 evening, Phase 1 (converter): standard progress line, branch phase1/progress-line

All local; nothing published. Track: Phase 1 (upstream-bound, conventional commits). Queue item E7, "converter heartbeat line".

- **What it does.** A conversion or `converter check` always shows one standard line that says what it is doing, and a log never goes quiet for more than the log interval (5 s).
  - Renderer (impl-1911): the status line ends with the current file or message (cut to 110 characters on a character boundary), a stage with no total prints `--%` plus the time in the stage, `tick` also prints a plain `[h:mm:ss.s]` heartbeat line off a terminal and in `--verbose`, and `converter check` uses the same line through a new `CheckProgress`.
  - Pipeline (impl-1912): Database reports one event per plugin parsed and names writing the database, validating it and writing the cell cache; Validating samples two atomic counters ten times a second (artifacts and bytes), so events follow the clock and not the artifact count; Publishing sends four named steps out of four; "Finalizing world database" opens the Validating stage; "Hashing plugin files" is named under Discovering.
  - No `ProgressStage` variant or `ProgressEvent` field changed, no serde output changed, nothing the converter produces changes. The launcher compiles unchanged.
- **Branch.** `phase1/progress-line`, two commits on origin/main 0f48d296: 9f545e69 (renderer and check) and 6adfd745 (pipeline). Its only checkout is `local/impl/impl-1912-p1-progress-silent-stages` (with its own target folder, class A); the #1911 worktree is removed.
- **Checked.** fmt clean; workspace clippy `--all-targets --all-features -D warnings` clean; workspace nextest 1167 of 1168 passed, the one failure being the known flaky `extraction_does_not_wait_for_a_front_end_that_stopped_reading` (it also failed once on the review of #1911, which does not touch extraction, and passed 5 of 5 alone on this branch); doctests clean; `cargo check -p launcher --all-targets` clean. The new timing tests ran 40 times in a row without a failure after two fixes (below).
- **Reviews.** Independent DeepSeek reviews of both parts. #1911: accept (one test added from its findings: the in-stage clock restarts when the stage changes). #1912: fix first, all taken: the sampler is now always stopped and joined, also when the run future is dropped (a test that fails without the guard, checked by mutation); the plugin checksum pass is named; two clock-based test bounds widened; my own reading added the stage-order fix for the finalizing event (it would have sent the front end back to the Database stage after LOD).
- **Not done, on purpose.**
  - `--reuse-assets` (`rebuild_metadata_async`, `metadata.rs`) still sends one event per stage and is silent in between; that is the same incident waiting on another path. Worth a follow-up brief.
  - LodChunks still has stage weight 0.0, so the overall bar sits still during LOD until a measured run gives it a weight.
  - `converter check` prints its heartbeat from its per-file callback, not from a timer, so a single huge file or the file walk before the first callback prints nothing.
  - Other bins (`texture-sync`, `asset-closure-*`) have their own lines.
  - Not seen on a real terminal yet: the tests cover the line text and the erase logic with a fake clock, not how a real console redraws it.
- **Next.** The user decides what happens to the branch. ## 2026-10-08 Phase 2 Dev, Hermes (impl-1059 + impl-1060 briefs: the two ranked crossing fixes)

Track: phase 2. Committed `tasks/impl-1059-p2-despawn-time-budget.md` and
`tasks/impl-1060-p2-preupdate-finalize-pacing.md` (383e5e99, on portal) — the fix briefs Upstream
Dev's ranked list was waiting on. **Correction to the re-profile report §7.1:** "drains over ~9
frames at the normal budget" is wrong — `max_cell_unloads_per_frame` defaults to **2** and the
demo passes no override, so a plain override-drop means a ~217-frame (~1.4 s) drain. impl-1059
therefore replaces the cell-count budget with a per-frame **time** budget
(`--max-unload-ms-per-frame`, default from the window's A/B, 0 = old unbudgeted path) and keeps
the overflow escape but trips it on *sustained backlog growth* (retire rate > drain rate), not on
a one-frame crossing burst — the escape's original intent (streaming.rs:1441-1447). Despawns ~70
drain frames of ~3 ms instead of one 136-205 ms Update burst; also shrinks the despawn share of
the render extract batch. impl-1060 paces the PreUpdate asset-finalize flush from the engine's
own side (bevy_asset's drain itself stays untouched — upstream patch rejected for now): a
`--max-cell-loads-in-flight` cap on `request_cell` issues, refused cells retried later, readiness
gate (`PortalPrewarm::settled`) unchanged so a crossing can never proceed half-loaded; expected
to remove the 297.5/173.6/152.7 ms PreUpdate worst, total load time paid inside the existing
prestream wait. Both briefs carry foundations answers, nextest test lists, build-gate acceptance,
and the coordination flags: impl-286 Stage B overlap (impl-1060 must re-scope if it lands
first) and the drain-visibility check (retired exteriors must be verified invisible during the
drain — RenderLayers claim from the re-profile §7.1, screenshot/probe in a walked run before
landing). Next: implementation worktrees for both, then the impl-287 window prices before/after.

is a per-frame take-back of exactly the published keys (never the portal's own hold), which the
`the_prewarm_reveals_the_destination_cell_and_takes_it_back_without_a_crossing` test pins both
ways; streaming.rs diff is empty. Fixed here: the Stage A report was cited by a name never
filed (`t286-…` → `portal-prestream-prewarm-mechanism-2026-10-08.md`) in the `PortalPrewarm` doc
and the handoff entry. Known review points for Upstream Dev: the window is read from
`PrestreamCells` directly (includes predicted beyond-radius doors, not radius-only); the warm-up
is readiness-bounded (cache settle, 2 flat frames, nothing waiting) and the tour's wait is held
adaptively with a 20 s cap; cells are revealed only on warm frames; one door at a time (nearest).
Reviewer's own gate run below.

## 2026-10-08 Portal Dev, Hermes (impl-286 Stage B: prestream pre-warm implemented)

Track: Portal. Nothing pushed. Branch `impl-286-portal-prestream-prewarm` commits d51773f0,
d79833b1, 90155163, worktree `D:/Mudcrab/worktrees/portal-impl-286`, on the portal lineage
(c9f2ffa7, includes impl-1058's spawn-time layer stamps). Implements Stage A's recommendation
(`local/research/portal-prestream-prewarm-mechanism-2026-10-08.md`): the pre-warm is the existing
portal camera activated behind the prestream window.

- **Mechanism.** `portal.rs` gains `PortalPrewarm` (resource, inited with the portal's own
  build) and `prewarm_destination_view`, chained in `PortalFrame` after `update_portal` and
  before the occlusion test/resize/leaf/isolation systems. Per frame: a doorway `update_portal`
  is really drawing marks its destination warmed; otherwise the nearest active-space door whose
  destination `plan_door_prestream` is streaming and not yet warmed becomes the target. While
  that destination's cells are still streaming the phase is `WaitingLoad` (the wait holds); once
  the landing cell is resident the phase is `Warming`: the portal camera is placed exactly where
  the doorway render places it (door_map pose, destination clip plane, main projection, full
  view, `sub_camera_view` cleared) and activated, spending the wait's frames on the 44-pipeline
  specialization burst and the destination's paced uploads. Deactivation is readiness-gated:
  `PipelineCounts` (the shared render-world bridge) read each frame; the view goes off the frame
  the created count has held flat `PREWARM_SETTLE_FRAMES` (2) with an empty waiting set. Warmed
  keys are remembered run-wide (a doorway the portal drew counts too), so re-approaches and
  windows that persist re-warm nothing.
- **Adaptive wait.** `demo_tour.rs` `Phase::Prestream` keeps its 6 s floor but holds past it
  while `PortalPrewarm::settled()` is false (destination still streaming in, or warm view
  running), capped at `PRESTREAM_WAIT_MAX_SECONDS` (20 s) like `Phase::Settle`'s ceiling; one
  log line names what held the wait when it ran >1 s past the floor. Walked runs need no timer:
  their window is `plan_door_prestream`'s own, and the warm view simply runs in it.
- **Foundations check.** No shortcuts: nothing loads faster or hides loading - the wait already
  exists and the work moves into it; `RenderAssetBytesPerFrame`/`upload_budget` untouched
  (tours run it unpaced today; noted, unchanged here). No scene/route knowledge: doors, cells
  and keys only, any worldspace. Destination sun off during warm-up (illuminance 0 while
  `update_destination_atmosphere` has no open doorway; no shadow cascades warmed); the warm view
  costs ~+1.6-2.5 ms render CPU per frame while the wait is open, named in the code. No
  `streaming.rs` touches; no timing run (AFTER numbers come from the booked re-profile window
  with impl-1058 and impl-285).
- **Tests** (headless, no GPU): `the_prewarm_draws_the_destination_behind_the_prestream_wait`
  (warm view runs behind the wait while the door is closed, publishes no doorway, deactivates on
  readiness, warmed destination never re-warmed); `the_prewarm_holds_the_prestream_wait_while_
  the_destination_loads` (WaitingLoad holds with an empty StreamingWorld, then warms when loads
  land); `a_run_without_a_prestream_window_is_never_warmed` (empty window and no-portal camera
  both idle, camera untouched). Three more were recovered from the worker's leftover working
  copy by the lead and committed as 5f3717a6: `the_prewarm_reveals_the_destination_cell_and_
  takes_it_back_without_a_crossing` (while the warm view runs the cell is stamped
  Destination/layer 2/visible - the crossing's own stamps - and every write is taken back when
  the wait ends without a crossing); `a_doorway_the_portal_is_drawing_takes_the_warm_state_down`
  (a really-drawn doorway stands the warm view down, clears the viewport, keeps the portal's
  own activation); `a_run_without_the_portal_prestreams_but_never_warms` (transition.rs:
  TransitionPlugin alone never gains PortalPrewarm). Existing transition/portal/tour tests
  unchanged and green.
- **Gated** (own build folder): fmt clean; gated clippy --workspace --all-targets --all-features
  -D warnings clean; gated nextest -p engine --all-targets 1131/1131 on the worker's four
  commits; the lead's watched re-run on the recovered tree (5f3717a6) in the same folder: fmt
  clean, gated clippy clean, gated nextest -p engine --all-targets **1134 passed / 4 skipped**.
- **Incident:** one stray `Viewport` import from outside this branch's work arrived in the
  working copy mid-run and was swept into d51773f0; removed in d79833b1. The lead saw the
  second occurrence: a botched re-indent of a transition test appeared in the working copy
  between two reads and was cleaned up again minutes later (stable since 05:22, sha-verified).
  Check concurrent writers before trusting a shared worktree's diff; no other session claimed
  this worktree.
- **Next:** hand to Upstream Dev for review; then the booked re-profile window prices impl-285 +
  impl-1058 + this together on the door-tour baseline.

## 2026-10-08 Phase 2 Dev, Hermes (impl-1057 ported onto the detail-tiered streamer: failed-cell retry with backoff)

Track: Phase 2. Nothing pushed. Branch `phase2/failed-cell-retry` commit **b2f2bea6** (from
portal c9f2ffa7), worktree `C:/d/Mudcrab/worktrees/p2-failed-cell-retry` (OWNER.txt; note the
`C:/d` path is an MSYS mangling slip - move/remove needs the user's yes), streaming.rs only,
+958/-95. Original GLM worker diff (detached at old base 6f583cd9) was partly superseded - portal
since landed the `full_failed` terrain rescue and the interior retry budget - so the leftover
apply was stashed (patch kept in the phase2-dev profile scratch) and the remaining gaps were
rebuilt per the revised brief `IMPL-1057-PORT-BRIEF.md` in the worktree, by a gated native
subagent, then re-verified by this session.

- `CellStatus::Failed` = `{ detail, attempts, reason, retry_at: Option<Instant>, replaced }`;
  `retry_at: None` = given up, never asked again while the entry lives; fresh entry on return
  (plan retain drops it, as before).
- `full_failed`: `HashSet<CellKey>` -> `HashMap<CellKey, FullFailure>` `{ attempts, reason,
  retry_at }` with `retryable(now)`; plan_cells re-asks Full once the entry is retryable
  (wall-clock backoff 1 s doubling to 2 s, `MAX_CELL_LOAD_ATTEMPTS` = 3), keeping the terrain
  rescue root drawn as `replaced` the whole time (no ground hole; the tier-swap machinery takes
  the root over); downgrade-to-terrain stays for pending/given-up entries.
- `record_failed_cell` (shared exteriors/interiors via `next_full_attempt`): one `warn!` per
  failure (key, cell id, attempt, reason); deterministic (LAND strict-validation at Full) or
  budget-spent goes straight to given up with one `error!` + `failed_cells_given_up`; else backoff.
  Ring/Terrain branches untouched (still empty-space accounting). Metrics: `failed_cell_retries`,
  `failed_cells_given_up` added beside `failed_cells`.
- Interior retry: budget unchanged (`INTERIOR_FAILED_RETRIES`), now additionally gated on the same
  wall-clock backoff; a given-up interior is never re-asked. NOTE: interiors effectively cap at
  `MAX_CELL_LOAD_ATTEMPTS` (3) via the shared give-up rule - one load TIGHTER than the old
  budget (old was 1 + 3 re-asks = 4; conservative direction, per Upstream Dev's impl-1057
  review), flag to review whether interiors should keep a separate give-up rule.
- Leak fix riding along: `unload_all_cells_now` and `validate_streaming_lifecycle` now account for
  `Failed { replaced }` roots (despawn on space change / counted as plan-accounted, not orphan).
- Deviation recorded: collider-build failure was NOT converted - at c9f2ffa7 it no longer fails
  the cell at all (`spawn_cell` draws the quadrant without a collider and commits), so there is no
  site; converting it would change behaviour the brief said to keep.
- Gated (own build folder `C:/Modding/Mudcrab/builds/p2-failed-cell-retry`, OWNER.txt): fmt clean;
  gated clippy `-p engine --all-targets --all-features -D warnings` clean (worker x5 + this
  session fresh); gated nextest `-p engine --no-fail-fast -E "test(streaming)"` **122/122** and
  full `-p engine --no-fail-fast` **1132 passed / 4 skipped** (worker) - this session re-ran
  streaming **122/122** fresh on the commit. Four acceptance tests (a)-(d) adapted to the tiered
  architecture plus the existing suite passing unchanged.
- Next: hand to Upstream Dev for review against `IMPL-1057-PORT-BRIEF.md`. When the user is
  awake: move/remove the `C:/d/...` worktree (needs consent), and the booked re-profile window
  still waits for impl-1058 + prestream pre-warm + impl-285 together.

## 2026-10-08 Portal Dev, Hermes (impl-285 implemented: crossing-frame attribution)

Track: Portal. Nothing pushed. Job: Upstream Dev's brief
`tasks/deepseek/impl-285-portal-crossing-frame-attribution.md` (84fd7f29), handed to this
session in the group chat. Implemented directly (not delegated): bounded, two owned files.

- **Done.** Branch `impl-285-portal-crossing-frame-attribution`, commit **9cb1fc80**, worktree
  `D:\Mudcrab\worktrees\portal-impl-285` (OWNER.txt), cut from portal (84fd7f29). Measurement
  only; no behaviour change.
- **`frame/remainder`** (`render_timing.rs:69` const, window in `end_main`:480 /
  `start_main`:465, `remainder_ms`:635): an `Instant` lap opened at the end of `Last`, closed
  at the next `First`, reported **minus the extract and the wait when both ran inside the
  window** (they are accumulated while the window is open, in `extracted`/`handed_over`), never
  negative. Written into `CycleSides.remainder_ms` and sampled as `frame/remainder` while
  recording. Gated on `track_cycles` (benchmark runs); zero cost otherwise.
- **Per-cycle render phases** (`CycleSides`: the six new phase fields; `lap`:510 writes each
  phase into the cycle the render frame belongs to via `phase_writer`:641): the seven phases are
  one value per cycle each. `render-cycles.csv` (`profiling.rs:531 render_cycles_csv`, written
  in `write_bundle`:830 beside `frame-series.csv`, bounded `MAX_RENDER_CYCLE_ROWS` = 100 000),
  header, in order:
  `cycle,main_world,extract,wait_for_render_thread,frame/remainder,render_thread,render/extract_commands_and_assets,render/specialize_and_views,render/queue,render/prepare,render/graph_and_present,render/cleanup,render/swapchain_acquire`
  **Join:** `render-cycles.csv` row `n` ↔ `frame-series.csv` frame `n` (same number). Row `n`'s
  `render_thread`/phases are the render schedule that ran *during* cycle `n` — fed by extract
  `n−1` when pipelined (the n → n+1 offset) — so a crossing frame's render time is explained by
  its own row's phases. `frame-series.csv` is byte-for-byte unchanged.
- **Row plumbing:** `RenderTimings` mirrors each cycle write into `cycle_rows` (retention keeps
  only cycles that can still be written); `take_completed_cycles`:387 hands over completed rows
  once (watermark so a late write cannot duplicate); a new `drain_render_cycles` system
  (`profiling.rs:261`, installed only when the run profiles, ordered after
  `start_main_world`, which became `pub(crate)`) moves them into `ProfilingState`.
  The run's last cycle or two have no row: their remainder window never closed.
- **Tests** (all new, `render_timing.rs` unless noted): `the_remainder_excludes_the_extract_and_wait_that_ran_inside_the_window` (pure),
  `the_cycle_remainder_excludes_the_extract_and_wait_that_ran_in_its_window` (record),
  `the_cycle_sides_account_for_the_whole_cycle` (identity, sleep-based),
  `the_remainder_is_kept_only_when_cycles_are_tracked`, `laps_write_their_phase_into_the_cycle_record` (both pipelined settings),
  `completed_cycle_rows_are_handed_over_once_once_they_are_complete`,
  `the_render_cycles_csv_names_every_column` and
  `the_render_cycles_series_keeps_its_most_recent_rows` (`profiling.rs`);
  `writes_complete_profile_bundle` now also asserts `render-cycles.csv`.
- **Checks** (own build folder `C:/Modding/Mudcrab/builds/portal-impl-285-target`, OWNER.txt):
  `cargo fmt --all -- --check` clean; gated `cargo clippy -p engine --all-targets
  --all-features -- -D warnings` clean; gated `cargo nextest run -p engine --all-targets`
  **1131 passed / 4 skipped**.
- **Deviations from the brief:** `write_cycle`'s writer changed `FnOnce`→`Fn` (it now writes two
  records); the rows drain runs in `First` after the main frame's start (a row is complete only
  once the next `First` closed its remainder) instead of inside the ledger's take — the ledger's
  records are untouched; the remainder is sampled into `cpu-spans.json` as `frame/remainder` on
  top of the CSV column (distribution for free, same gate).
- **Foundations check:** no shortcuts (measurement only, nothing hidden, no behaviour change);
  foundationally optimised (zero cost when not tracking/profiling, bounded stores); flexible
  (no worldspace/scene assumptions — the window is measured for any route); moddable (nothing
  vanilla-specific); aligned (the 160 fps goal's measurement prerequisite — the ~80 ms crossing
  remainder becomes attributable, and the per-cycle phases make the pipeline-activity frames
  comparable, which will price the prestream pre-warm's buy-back). Shortcut question: the only
  thing this takes short is the run's final row (window never closes) — documented, and it
  cannot hide a crossing (crossings are mid-run).
- Nothing running; no timing run (the verifying re-profile is a booked window after this lands
  together with the prestream pre-warm).

### 2026-09-23 (day) - Claude (the Riverwood demo, and two rendering bugs it exposed)

**The user's ask:** "start on the riverwood demo", off research-055's data
(`docs/design/riverwood-demo.md`). Section 8 of that document is the part written after the demo
was built and run, and is the part to trust about behaviour.

**The demo works.** `--demo riverwood --walk` starts on the Helgen road south-west of the village;
`play-riverwood-demo.cmd` is beside the Blackreach launchers. `--demo-tour` is no longer
Alftand-only (impl-056): a route is keyed off the `--demo` name, and Riverwood's is eight doors -
four houses, each entered and left again. The passing run is `local/demo/riverwood2/tour.txt`:
**8 crossings of 8, all by walking, 0 dark frames in 2,056 captured frames.** The seamless
crossing now holds in daylight and in both directions, which the Alftand route could not show.
One tour bug fixed on the way: the walk-through's standoffs were 60 and 320 units, and inside
Alvor's House the far wall is nearer than 320; a third, 160, is tried last.

**Two rendering bugs, both dataset-wide, both found by looking at Riverwood:**

1. **The forest was invisible** (research-059, impl-063, `abaa14f`). Pines rendered as a spray of
   dots. My hypothesis - alpha-tested foliage falling through `render.rs`'s `AlphaMode::Blend`
   fallback - was **wrong**, and the worker falsified it properly: the trees convert to `MASK`
   with the NIF's own threshold. Bevy tests `vertex_color.a * texture.a` against the cutoff, and
   Skyrim's tree NIFs carry a `COLOR_0` whose alpha is a shader parameter, not opacity. Fixed
   engine-side at mesh load, no reconversion. Proof: `local/reference/_tree-before-after.png` -
   the canopies fill in and the house, thatch and wall in the same frame do not move. This also
   un-breaks `snow.wgsl`, which multiplies coverage by `in.color.a` under a comment claiming no
   converted mesh has vertex colours; 1,337 of them do.
2. **Everything read wet** (research-058, websearch-060, impl-062 `59f1f20`, impl-064 running).
   `material.rs` mapped Skyrim's Blinn-Phong glossiness *exponent* as a linear percentage. All
   59,268 published materials were wrong; 14,825 were clamped to Bevy's roughness floor. Now
   `(2/(n+2))^0.25`. The other half - the specular mask in the normal map's alpha, which the
   converter drops - is impl-064; websearch-060 settled that the mask scales intensity, not the
   exponent, so it can be a `specularTexture` pointing at the normal map with no derived textures.

**Converter schema 17 -> 18.** impl-064 rides the same bump, so one reconversion covers both. The
first reconversion was running when this was written (`local/conversion-s18.out.log`); a second is
needed after impl-064 lands. **Until a reconversion completes, the engine refuses the assets on
disk** - that is its schema check working, not a fault.

**Also landed:** impl-057, `crates/engine/src/model_animation.rs` - a model's own looping `Idle`
clip on every non-door reference. The mill wheel the Riverwood finale looks at now turns (16.67 s
a revolution, confirmed in the log). The snow catalogue read `material_object IS NOT NULL`, but
the exporter writes a plain 0 for a static with no `MATO`: 9,924 markers, fences and road chunks
against 2,506 real snow statics. Now `> 0`, with a test that fails on the old query (`ad7f138`).

**Reference shots.** `tools/reference/riverwood_shots.json`, twelve poses, ten against a UESP
reference in `local/reference/riverwood/`. All twelve render. research-055 computed the eight
exteriors at ground + 120 (eye height), but UESP's "place" screenshots are free-camera shots from
above the rooftops, so four of them could not frame their reference at any yaw. A sweep of
elevated candidates is `local/reference/rw_sweep4/`; the mill finale has been re-fitted from it.
The inn and the whole-village aerial still need one more pass - `local/reference/rw_sweep2.json`
is written and unrendered.

**Running when this was written:** impl-064 (the specular mask), doc-065 (the demo guide covering
both demos). **Next:** finish the reconversion, re-render and check the wet look is gone, land
impl-064, reconvert again, re-fit the inn and village poses, then a regression run of the Alftand
tour.
